# 041 — Bitcoin Cash Fork

**Date:** 2017-08-01T13:16:00Z  ·  **Era:** Mania and rebuilding

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-E0034` → `LORE-EVT-0034` / `E0034`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

Bitcoin Cash split from Bitcoin after the last common Bitcoin block, height 478,558, on 1 August 2017; BCH then followed rules allowing larger blocks.

**Why it earns a card:** The scaling conflict produces a lasting competing chain.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A crowded Bitcoin road divides into two routes after a long argument over carrying capacity. Both groups continue with shared historical cargo; one route opens wider lanes while the other follows a different scaling path.

**Composition:** View from the common road looking toward the fork; neither branch disappears or becomes a villain's path.

**Mood:** Determined disagreement; amber common ground with diverging accents.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not imply holders had to choose only one resulting balance, call either chain a counterfeit, or depict this as the SegWit activation date.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Shared paving

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Identical paving stones extend up to the junction.

**Meaning:** Represents the common transaction history before the chain split.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Wider loading bay

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** The Bitcoin Cash route includes a visibly wider loading bay.

**Meaning:** A metaphor for larger blocks, not a claim of unlimited throughput.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Two route petitions

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Opposing annotated route plans are pinned to the same noticeboard.

**Meaning:** Expresses the social governance conflict without inventing quotations from named participants.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Folded sidecar plan

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small diagram of a detachable witness compartment rests on the other branch's desk.

**Meaning:** A deliberate nearby-story connection to SegWit's later activation, not a claim the two events occurred together.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [042 — SegWit Activates](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/042-segwit-activates.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

**Composition:** Foreground notice reflected in a shutter, with subdued stalls receding behind it.

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

### 3. Unfinished sales pitch

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

## The moment

OneCoin founder Ruja Ignatova traveled from Sofia to Athens on 25 October 2017 and has not been seen publicly since; she had been federally charged earlier that month.

**Why it earns a card:** A human focal point for the OneCoin fraud story.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** An airport departure board and an abandoned presentation podium frame the disappearance of OneCoin promoter Ruja Ignatova. Her whereabouts remain outside the image: the absence is the subject.

**Composition:** An empty foreground chair and closed luggage dominate; the departure board is a secondary factual anchor.

**Mood:** Unease and unanswered questions; glamour drained into muted airport light.

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

# 051 — Lightning Torch

**Date:** 2019-01-19 to 2019-04  ·  **Era:** Mania and rebuilding

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2019-006` → `LORE-CAN-2019-006` / `C2019-006`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

The Lightning Torch began on 19 January 2019 and was passed between hundreds of participants across dozens of countries before reaching its protocol/payment limit.

**Why it earns a card:** A relay of payments creates a participatory global story.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A Lightning payment moves hand to hand as a glowing relay torch around a table that bends like a world map. The focus is trust among strangers and the practical limits of a shared experiment.

**Composition:** Circular handoff composition with one clear current holder; distant hands fade into map contours.

**Mood:** Friendly suspense; warm flame inside cool blue channels.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not claim every country participated, draw a literal Olympic event, or show an unlimited payment size.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Growing handle bands

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Small added bands accumulate on the torch handle as it passes.

**Meaning:** References participants increasing the payment during the relay; no exact increment without source checking.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Channel-width gate

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A narrow gate appears farther along the route.

**Meaning:** Hints at Lightning payment and routing constraints that bounded the experiment.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Mismatched gloves

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Different ordinary gloves surround the table.

**Meaning:** Symbolises strangers cooperating across locations without inventing named participants.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Beta station stamp

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A worn station stamp repeats the Lightning beta card's channel latch.

**Meaning:** Connects the community demonstration to the earlier technology milestone.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [048 — Lightning Goes Live](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/048-lightning-goes-live.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

# 052 — QuadrigaCX

**Date:** 2019-01 to 2019-04  ·  **Era:** Mania and rebuilding

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2019-005` → `LORE-CAN-2019-005` / `C2019-005`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

QuadrigaCX shut down after founder Gerald Cotten's reported death and entered court-supervised proceedings. Later OSC review concluded the exchange owed clients roughly C$215 million with almost no assets available and that the shortfall resulted largely from Cotten's fraud and mismanagement, not simply inaccessible cold-wallet keys.

**Why it earns a card:** A notorious exchange collapse with an extraordinary missing-key narrative.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** Customers face an exchange safe whose locked-door story does not explain the damaged ledgers behind it. The composition should convey a collapse involving fraud and mismanagement as well as the reported death of its operator.

**Composition:** Foreground customer claim folders, a closed safe to one side, and torn accounting records visible behind it.

**Mood:** Distrust and grief without sensationalism; cold office light.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not reduce the losses to one missing password, allege a faked death, or invent a discovered master key or victim portrait.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Unreconciled ledger

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Two ledger pages fail to align across the desk.

**Meaning:** References the regulator's later findings of fraud and mismanagement; mark as retrospective interpretation.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Customer claims box

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A box of claim forms sits outside the safe rather than inside it.

**Meaning:** Represents customer liabilities and recovery proceedings, not physical holdings inside a vault.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Misleading key shadow

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small key casts a shadow too short to reach the ledger.

**Meaning:** Shows why the lost-access explanation alone is inadequate; the object is fictional.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Rain-marked withdrawal slip

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A slip echoes the damp pending receipt from Mt. Gox.

**Meaning:** A thematic connection between failed exchanges, without asserting identical causes or legal outcomes.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [024 — Mt. Gox](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/024-mt-gox.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

## The moment

During the global COVID-19 market panic, Bitcoin fell violently alongside traditional risk assets, dropping below $6,000 and later below $5,000 during the wider selloff.

**Why it earns a card:** A defining cross-market crash during the pandemic.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A global market storm sweeps through a crypto trading room during the early pandemic sell-off. Falling screens and strained liquidation machinery share the frame; there is no single villain pulling a lever.

**Composition:** Wide room with one small observer and several linked markets visible through windows.

**Mood:** Alarm and disorientation; cyan screens under storm-grey light.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not attribute every decline to one cause, use a later day's low as this day's exact price, or show a fictional trader as a real victim.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Liquidation chute

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small collateral tray tips into an automatic settlement chute.

**Meaning:** References stress in DeFi liquidation systems without claiming all platforms behaved identically.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Empty safety chair

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A chair has rolled away from an emergency console.

**Meaning:** Symbolises perceived loss of control rather than an actual exchange evacuation.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Spring seed tray

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A few unopened seed packets remain dry beneath the desk.

**Meaning:** A deliberate foreshadowing of DeFi Summer after the March shock.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

## Illustration brief

**Composition:** Two clear paths enter a shared lending garden; rewards sit on a separate shelf, distinct from interest.

**Mood:** Excitement with visible complexity; saturated green and violet.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not portray risk-free yield, confuse governance tokens with deposited principal, or imply one date accounts for all of DeFi Summer.

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

## The moment

**Why it earns a card:** An emblematic community-led token distribution and yield-farming story.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A community finds a small, initially limited batch of governance tokens left on an open workshop table. There is no privileged founder's throne; the scene captures the unusual initial distribution and collective rush.

**Composition:** Top-down table with ordinary participants around all sides, no central owner figure.

**Mood:** Improvised collaboration; blue ink, warm wood and evening light.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not describe the initial 30,000 supply as an eternal cap, imply no later governance changes, or promise holders investment returns.

### 3. Shared wrench rack

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Every side of the table can reach the same tools.

**Meaning:** Represents participation and governance without claiming power was perfectly equal in practice.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

# 060 — The Vampire Attack

**Date:** 2020-08-28 to 2020-09-09  ·  **Era:** Mania and rebuilding

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2020-034` → `LORE-CAN-2020-034` / `C2020-034`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

SushiSwap launched as a Uniswap fork with SUSHI liquidity-mining incentives and then migrated more than $800 million of liquidity away from Uniswap in a 'vampire attack.'

**Why it earns a card:** A memorable competitive liquidity migration.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A playful sushi counter offers incentives that persuade liquidity providers to move assets from a neighbouring exchange pool. Make the movement voluntary and strategic: the vampire image is a metaphor for competition.

**Composition:** Two adjacent pools connected by a clearly open migration route, with providers carrying trays themselves.

**Mood:** Mischievous rivalry; magenta and warm kitchen light.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not depict a hack, stolen user funds, literal vampires attacking people, or every stage of the episode as occurring on one day.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Migration trays

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Liquidity providers carry labelled pool-share trays toward the new counter.

**Meaning:** References moving liquidity rather than unauthorised extraction.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Reward menu

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small menu offers SUSHI incentives beside the new pool.

**Meaning:** Shows how token rewards helped attract providers; rates require date-specific checking before printing.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Vegetarian vampire charm

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A tiny smiling fang charm hangs from a chef's utensil rack.

**Meaning:** A harmless visual pun on the industry's vampire-attack term, not an allegation of theft.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Familiar pool tile

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** One tile beside the route repeats the original Uniswap workshop's twin-pool pattern.

**Meaning:** Makes the origin of the migrated liquidity discoverable as a collection connection.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [049 — Uniswap Launches](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/049-uniswap-launches.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

**Why it earns a card:** The proof-of-stake chain lays groundwork for The Merge.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A new proof-of-stake lighthouse begins shining beside Ethereum's still-working mining harbour. The two systems coexist; the viewer can see a future connection but it is not yet made.

**Composition:** Two distinct structures share the coastline, separated by water and an unfinished connecting span.

**Mood:** Patient anticipation; violet dusk and a clear beacon beam.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not show proof of work ending in 2020, claim execution moved to the Beacon Chain at genesis, or present staking as risk-free.

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Meaning:** Shows coexistence with Ethereum's proof-of-work execution chain before the Merge.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Unfinished bridge anchor

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** An unused bridge anchor rests at the water's edge.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

## The moment

Buterin sent roughly 410 trillion SHIB to an inaccessible burn address after receiving an enormous allocation from the token's creators.

**Why it earns a card:** A large unsolicited token allocation meets an unexpected response.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** An enormous unsolicited pile of meme tokens is sent toward an inaccessible address, while a separate charitable gift route remains visibly distinct. The action is a deliberate disposal of token control, not ordinary coin spending.

**Composition:** Two clearly separated paths leave a token pile: the focal path ends at a sealed dark aperture, the smaller path at a donation envelope.

**Mood:** Decisive restraint; charcoal, ember orange and calm violet.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not confuse the burn with the separate charity donation, imply all supply was burned, or display a usable address as a payment instruction.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Meaning:** Explains that the tokens had been sent to Vitalik Buterin without being a normal purchase.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. No-return address slot

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** The main path ends in a slot with no opening handle.

**Meaning:** A metaphor for an inaccessible burn address, not destruction of blockchain records.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Extinguished match

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A tiny match has already gone out beside the aperture.

**Meaning:** A quiet visual pun on burning without portraying literal destruction of physical money.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Separate aid envelope

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** The smaller route contains a relief envelope with a different design from the burned pile.

**Meaning:** A thematic connection to crypto donations; it must not imply these were the later Ukraine donations.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

# 069 — EIP-1559

**Date:** 2021-08-05T12:33:42Z  ·  **Era:** Mainstream boom and contagion

## The moment

Ethereum activated the London upgrade at block 12,965,000, introducing EIP-1559's base-fee mechanism and fee burning.

**Why it earns a card:** Ethereum changes fee mechanics and introduces a base-fee burn.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** Ethereum's fee mechanism gains a visible split: a base portion disappears into a controlled furnace while another route remains available to the block producer. Make a protocol change feel mechanical and precise.

**Composition:** Cutaway fee booth with two distinct exits; the main flame is restrained rather than apocalyptic.

**Mood:** Orderly redesign; amber fire inside violet machinery.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not show every fee burned, promise permanently falling supply, or depict this as the end of mining.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Base-fee chute

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** One clearly marked fee portion feeds the small furnace.

**Meaning:** References EIP-1559's base-fee burn.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Separate tip drawer

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A second small drawer remains outside the burn path.

**Meaning:** Shows that other fee components are not simply all destroyed; exact post-upgrade mechanics need technical review if diagrammed.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Moving gauge

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A fee gauge has a movable needle rather than a fixed cheap-price sticker.

**Meaning:** Signals a changing fee mechanism, not a guarantee of low costs.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Future engine socket

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** An unused engine socket repeats the approved Merge spacecraft's connector shape.

**Meaning:** Foreshadows the later consensus transition while keeping London and the Merge distinct.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

# 070 — The Poly Network Exploit

**Date:** 2021-08-10  ·  **Era:** Mainstream boom and contagion

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2021-072` → `LORE-CAN-2021-072` / `C2021-072`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

An attacker exploited Poly Network and stole about $612 million across multiple chains.

**Why it earns a card:** A huge exploit followed by an unusual return of funds.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A cross-chain bridge loses a vast cargo of tokens, then receives much of it back along an uncertain return path. Use two visual beats separated by light so the recovery is not mistaken for simultaneous movement.

**Composition:** Broken bridge near the foreground and a later, quieter return dock in the distance.

**Mood:** Shock followed by uneasy relief; electric blue with caution amber.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not portray the attacker as a heroic identified figure, assume every loss was immediately reversed, or call this an Ethereum consensus failure.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Multiple cargo seals

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Different token-shaped seals appear on separate cargo crates.

**Meaning:** References assets taken across several chains rather than one homogeneous coin pile.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Return manifest

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

# 071 — Bitcoin Becomes Legal Tender

**Date:** 2021-09-07  ·  **Era:** Mainstream boom and contagion

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2021-080` → `LORE-CAN-2021-080` / `C2021-080`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

El Salvador's Bitcoin Law entered into force in September 2021, making Bitcoin legal tender alongside the US dollar under the law then in effect.

**Why it earns a card:** El Salvador's law takes effect, a defining sovereign-adoption moment.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** An ordinary shop in El Salvador displays both dollar and bitcoin payment choices on the day the law takes effect. Centre everyday use and uncertainty rather than a leader portrait or utopian city.

**Composition:** Shop-counter view with customer and merchant considering two distinct payment routes.

**Mood:** Historic experiment grounded in daily life; sunlit colour with thoughtful expressions.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not imply everyone used bitcoin willingly, confuse June passage with September effect, or describe the 2021 regime as unchanged today.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Two payment choices

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small counter sign offers both USD and BTC.

**Meaning:** Represents legal tender alongside the dollar at the selected historical moment.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Effective-date page

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A calendar page is folded behind a policy notice.

**Meaning:** Distinguishes the law taking effect from its earlier legislative passage; exact date comes from the selection record.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Merchant's pencil

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** An ordinary pencil lies between two pricing columns.

**Meaning:** Shows practical work in adapting payments, not a claim of fixed exchange rates.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Reserve cupboard silhouette

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A distant cupboard carries a discreet government-reserve motif.

**Meaning:** A forward thematic connection to a different country's later reserve policy, not the same legal mechanism.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [099 — The US Bitcoin Reserve](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/099-the-us-bitcoin-reserve.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

# 072 — China's Mining Crackdown

**Date:** 2021-09-24  ·  **Era:** Mainstream boom and contagion

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2021-085` → `LORE-CAN-2021-085` / `C2021-085`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

On 24 September, authorities published a nationwide mining notice dated 3 September 2021. This formalised a crackdown already under way earlier that year.

**Why it earns a card:** A major intervention reshapes Bitcoin's mining geography.

**Date qualification:** 24 September is publication of a notice dated 3 September. Earlier May/June restrictions and mining migration must not be presented as beginning in September.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** Mining equipment is packed into transit crates as official restrictions reshape China's mining industry. The composition combines an administrative notice with relocation, making clear that the public notice formalised a process already under way.

**Composition:** Warehouse foreground with unplugged rigs and open shipping crates; the notice is secondary but visible.

**Mood:** Dislocation and adaptation; industrial grey with distant sunrise.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not date the entire crackdown to one September day, claim all mining ended forever, or confuse mining restrictions with a worldwide Bitcoin shutdown.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Earlier packing labels

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Some crates have older month tabs partly visible beneath the newest notice.

**Meaning:** Acknowledges earlier stages of the 2021 crackdown rather than a single-day origin.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Unplugged power distribution

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A disconnected power rail runs beneath the stacked rigs.

**Meaning:** Represents the operational impact on mining sites without rendering an unsafe real wiring plan.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Unfinished destination label

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A shipping label has an empty destination line.

**Meaning:** Expresses uncertainty and relocation without inventing a particular operator's route.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Old offering notice

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A folded finance notice echoes the ICO-ban card in a filing box.

**Meaning:** Connects separate regulatory episodes while preserving their different subjects and dates.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [043 — China Bans ICOs](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/043-china-bans-icos.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

# 073 — Taproot

**Date:** 2021-11-14  ·  **Era:** Mainstream boom and contagion

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2021-100` → `LORE-CAN-2021-100` / `C2021-100`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

Taproot's new Bitcoin consensus rules activated at block 709,632 after locking in through the Speedy Trial activation process.

**Why it earns a card:** A substantial Bitcoin upgrade changes signature and scripting capabilities.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** Bitcoin's upgrade appears as a carefully grafted new branch on an established cryptographic tree. Existing roots remain intact while selected spending paths become more elegant and flexible.

**Composition:** Close view of the graft with the full sturdy trunk visible; no giant unrelated growth explosion.

**Mood:** Patient craftsmanship; muted green and amber.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not promise universal transaction anonymity, depict a separate coin launch, or imply every user instantly adopted the new spending type.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Schnorr tool roll

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small roll of signature tools sits beneath the graft.

**Meaning:** References Schnorr signatures within Taproot; exact equations are unnecessary decorative risk.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Hidden branch sleeve

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** One branch is partly concealed inside a neat protective sleeve.

**Meaning:** A simplified metaphor for revealing less unused script information, not a promise that all spending details vanish.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Old root marker

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** An older marker remains firmly attached near the trunk's base.

**Meaning:** Shows continuity through an upgrade rather than replacement of Bitcoin's history.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Witness-compartment charm

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A tiny charm repeats SegWit's connected compartment motif.

**Meaning:** Links two upgrade milestones without equating their mechanisms.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [042 — SegWit Activates](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/042-segwit-activates.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

# 074 — ConstitutionDAO

**Date:** 2021-11-18  ·  **Era:** Mainstream boom and contagion

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2021-103` → `LORE-CAN-2021-103` / `C2021-103`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

ConstitutionDAO raised roughly $47 million from thousands of contributors to bid for a rare first printing of the US Constitution but lost the Sotheby's auction; the document sold for $43.2 million.

**Why it earns a card:** An internet collective attempts to buy a historical document at auction.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** An online crowd pools funds for a rare Constitution auction, then watches the winning lot move beyond reach. Keep the funded crowd and the lost bid in the same story; participation did not guarantee acquisition.

**Composition:** Crowd-lit screens in foreground, a distant auction document behind glass, and a closed return desk at one edge.

**Mood:** Collective ambition followed by disappointment; parchment gold against digital violet.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not say the DAO bought the document, equate gross funds raised with bidding capacity, or show a fictional bidder as the actual winner.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Unclaimed display stand

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** The group's prepared display stand remains empty.

**Meaning:** Makes the auction loss unambiguous.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Fee envelope

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small costs envelope sits between the treasury and the bidding paddle.

**Meaning:** Hints that gross funds and a practical maximum bid differ; exact expenses require source verification.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Governance meeting sketch

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small sketch echoes the two competing maps from The Split.

**Meaning:** A thematic link to coordination and collective decisions, not a technical connection between the projects.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

Coinbase aired a 60-second bouncing-QR-code Super Bowl ad that generated more than 20 million landing-page hits in one minute and forced the company to throttle traffic briefly.

**Why it earns a card:** A minimalist Super Bowl advert becomes a mainstream crypto moment.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A minimal bouncing square interrupts the spectacle of a major television advert break. Viewers reach for phones as an almost empty screen becomes more memorable than a crowded commercial.

**Composition:** Living-room view with a deliberately simple television rectangle; the moving-square trail gives the composition its rhythm.

**Mood:** Curiosity and playful surprise; saturated screen colour in a dark room.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not include a second functional QR code, recreate a valid signup destination, or imply all visitors became customers.

### 2. Overloaded doorway

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Meaning:** Represents the traffic surge and throttling; avoid unsourced live counters.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Untouched snack bowl

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Meaning:** A small joke about attention shifting from the game to the advert.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

# 077 — Crypto for Ukraine

## The moment

Ukraine's government said official crypto-donation channels had helped raise more than $53 million by 3 March following Russia's invasion.

**Why it earns a card:** Crypto fundraising becomes part of a real international crisis.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A stream of small digital donations reaches a relief coordination desk during the early response to Russia's invasion of Ukraine. Focus on rapid coordination and practical aid supplies, with no identifiable victims or celebratory combat imagery.

**Composition:** Hands checking incoming contributions at a table, with packed aid boxes beyond a window.

**Mood:** Urgent solidarity; restrained blue and yellow within the established illustration style.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not show a working donation address, imply all reported funds bought particular supplies, or confuse the fundraising milestone with the invasion's start.

# 078 — The Ronin Hack

## The moment

The Ronin bridge exploit became public on 29 March. Attackers had taken 173,600 ETH and 25.5 million USDC after compromising validator keys.

**Why it earns a card:** A defining bridge failure links gaming adoption to infrastructure risk.

**Date qualification:** Timeline anchors the 29 March disclosure. Contemporary reporting dates the attack to 23 March; the project's later reopening post conflates the dates. Resolve the original incident record before approving a card date for the attack itself.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A bridge guarded by a limited set of signing stations is breached after enough keys fall under attacker control. The public discovery is the focal moment: operators confront the drained route after the attack.

**Composition:** Dark bridge with several compromised gate controls, observed from a later incident-response desk.

**Mood:** Delayed shock; violet night and warning amber.

**Factual boundaries:** Use 29 March as the disclosure anchor; do not silently replace it with the attack date, portray the exploit as gameplay, or reveal usable credentials.

**Meaning:** Preserves the two asset types in the reported theft rather than merging them into one coin pile.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Late alarm shadow

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** An alarm casts a shadow toward an earlier, empty watch position.

**Meaning:** A metaphor for delayed discovery, not an invented precise detection timeline.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Loaned creature roster

# 079 — The Depeg

**Date:** 2022-05-09  ·  **Era:** Mainstream boom and contagion

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. Approved art preserved; optional revisions need separate approval.

## The moment

TerraUSD (UST) lost its dollar peg in May 2022 and collapsed toward zero.

**Why it earns a card:** The UST failure is a central stablecoin disaster; existing approved card.

**Date qualification:** Uses the approved card's 9 May milestone within the wider May depeg sequence; this is not a claim that every initial fluctuation began on that day.

**Approved interpretation to preserve:** Fictional city and observer. Shattered moon symbolises LUNA collapse; UST and severed $1 cable symbolise loss of the dollar peg. Anchor sign references advertised interest up to 20%. No real moon, physical reserve/collateral link, literal location or actual person is depicted. 09 MAY 2022 dates the reassurance post during the multi-day collapse.

**Approved date / rarity:** 2022-05-09 / rare.

**Locked artwork:** [cards/crypto/season-01/the-depeg-master-01/art.png](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/the-depeg-master-01/art.png).

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** Preserve the approved Depeg scene: a fictional observer beneath a shattered moon, a severed UST dollar tether and the ANCHOR sign. The selected date anchors the reassurance post during a collapse that unfolded over several days.

**Composition:** Keep the existing city, broken cable and unlettered moon hierarchy.

**Mood:** Preserve the approved ominous palette.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not add LUNA lettering to the moon, imply literal dollar collateral was severed, or present the whole collapse as a one-day event.

Ethereum's Paris upgrade activated on 15 September 2022, merging mainnet execution with Beacon Chain proof-of-stake consensus and ending proof-of-work mining.

**Why it earns a card:** Ethereum changes consensus mechanism; existing approved card.

**Approved interpretation to preserve:** Original fictional spacecraft; visual interpretation of the official engine-swap analogy. Not a historical vehicle or reconstruction.

**Approved date / rarity:** 2022-09-15 / mythic.

**Locked artwork:** [cards/crypto/season-01/the-merge-master-02/art.png](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/the-merge-master-02/art.png).

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** Preserve the approved fictional spacecraft changing engines in flight, based on Ethereum's engine-swap metaphor. The craft continues its journey while proof of work gives way to proof of stake.

**Composition:** Retain the locked spacecraft, engine swap and composition.

**Mood:** Preserve the approved cinematic lighting.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not claim the vehicle is real, the Merge eliminated transaction fees, or all promised scaling changes arrived that day.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Old engine tow line

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Candidate placement: a safely detached old engine retains a faint mining-grid motif.

**Meaning:** References the end of Ethereum proof of work, not the end of all crypto mining.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Validator ignition ring

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Candidate placement: multiple small ignition points surround the new engine.

**Meaning:** Represents proof-of-stake validator participation; not a literal validator count.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Unbroken flight trail

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Candidate placement: a continuous thin trail remains behind the spacecraft during the swap.

**Meaning:** A metaphor for continuity of the network transition, not a promise that every service had zero disruption.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Beacon navigation light

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Candidate placement: a small light repeats the earlier Beacon Chain lighthouse motif.

**Meaning:** Connects the 2020 consensus-chain launch to its 2022 integration with execution.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [062 — Beacon Chain](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/062-beacon-chain.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

# 084 — FTX Falls

**Date:** 2022-11-11  ·  **Era:** Mainstream boom and contagion

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2022-075` → `LORE-CAN-2022-075` / `C2022-075`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

FTX Trading and more than 100 affiliated entities filed for Chapter 11 bankruptcy protection in Delaware on 11 November 2022.

**Why it earns a card:** The defining exchange collapse of the 2022 crisis.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** An exchange's bright trading frontage goes dark while customers gather claim documents outside. Through the glass, a tangle of affiliated-company folders suggests the scale of the bankruptcy without pretending the illustration proves every allegation.

**Composition:** Customer-level view of a closed exchange facade, with paperwork and an extinguished sign as the focal elements.

**Mood:** Betrayal and uncertainty; cold cyan fading into darkness.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not date every revelation or arrest to 11 November, invent a criminal conviction on filing day, or reduce all affiliated entities to one legal company.

**Meaning:** Anchors the selected date to bankruptcy proceedings in Delaware.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Affiliated folder stack

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Many distinct company folders share the same filing tray.

**Meaning:** References the large group of affiliates in the proceedings without inventing a full corporate map.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Balance-screen reflection

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A phone still shows an abstract balance reflected in dark glass.

**Meaning:** Illustrates the difference between displayed assets and customer access, without using a real account.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Earlier queue token

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small token echoes Celsius's closed withdrawal window.

**Meaning:** A collection connection between customer-access failures, not an assertion of identical business models or recoveries.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Why it earns a card:** Bitcoin becomes the centre of a new inscription and collecting debate.

**Scene:** An artist inscribes a small digital artifact onto a satoshi represented as a marked grain moving along Bitcoin's existing chain. The innovation is a new use of familiar infrastructure, with debate visible around it.

**Mood:** Inventive and contentious; amber with sharp new colour accents.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not show a new blockchain launch, imply physical objects are literally inside coins, or settle contested claims about what Bitcoin should be used for.

# 086 — USDC Loses Its Peg

**Date:** 2023-03-10 to 2023-03-13  ·  **Era:** Recovery and institutions

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2023-021` → `LORE-CAN-2023-021` / `C2023-021`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

After Silicon Valley Bank failed, Circle disclosed that $3.3 billion of USDC reserves were held at the bank; USDC traded below $1 over the weekend before recovering after US authorities protected depositors.

**Why it earns a card:** Banking risk briefly destabilises a major dollar token.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A stablecoin reserve file is trapped behind a failed bank's closed window while the token's dollar gauge briefly drifts. A later reopening glow at the edge signals recovery without erasing the weekend's uncertainty.

**Composition:** Bank window and reserve ledger in the foreground, separate peg gauge to the side.

**Mood:** Temporary alarm easing toward relief; bank-grey and blue-green.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not portray USDC as algorithmic like UST, claim all reserves were at Silicon Valley Bank, or present the brief depeg as permanent.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Partial reserve tab

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Only one section of a larger reserve binder sits behind the closed window.

**Meaning:** References Circle's disclosed $3.3 billion exposure as part of its reserves, not the whole reserve pool.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Recovery return line

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A thin return line brings the peg gauge back toward its reference after the main scene.

**Meaning:** Represents later recovery alongside deposit-protection measures; mark the composition as a multi-day sequence.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Weekend desk lamp

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** An office lamp remains on beside a closed weekend calendar.

**Meaning:** Symbolises an unusually tense weekend without inventing an exact emergency meeting.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Different tether sample

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A broken cable sample from The Depeg lies in a separate comparison drawer.

**Meaning:** A collection contrast between two depegs; it must not imply identical backing, severity or outcome.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [079 — The Depeg](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/079-the-depeg.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

# 089 — The XRP Ruling

**Date:** 2023-07-13  ·  **Era:** Recovery and institutions

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2023-053` → `LORE-CAN-2023-053` / `C2023-053`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

In SEC v. Ripple Labs, the district court ruled that Ripple's institutional XRP sales under written contracts were securities transactions, while programmatic exchange sales did not satisfy Howey on the record before the court.

**Why it earns a card:** A nuanced court decision becomes a major industry moment.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A court decision splits two types of XRP sales into separate legal folders. The visual point is the distinction in the ruling, not a sweeping victory banner declaring every crypto transaction exempt.

**Composition:** A judge's desk seen without an identifiable face; two folders lead to different annotated outcomes.

**Mood:** Careful relief mixed with complexity; parchment and restrained blue.

# 090 — Binance Settles

**Date:** 2023-11-21  ·  **Era:** Recovery and institutions

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2023-079` → `LORE-CAN-2023-079` / `C2023-079`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

Binance pleaded guilty to federal charges and agreed to pay more than $4 billion in coordinated US resolutions; founder and CEO Changpeng Zhao separately pleaded guilty to violating the Bank Secrecy Act and resigned.

**Why it earns a card:** A major exchange reaches extensive US government settlements.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A major exchange signs a coordinated legal resolution while its founder's separate plea and departure are recorded in another folder. Keep the institution and individual distinct, with the paperwork carrying the story.

**Composition:** Two separate signing positions on one long table, neither presented as a theatrical courtroom confession.

**Mood:** Consequences and institutional change; sober black, white and muted yellow.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not portray imprisonment on the plea date, merge the company and individual charges, or call the combined resolutions a single unexplained fine.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Company resolution stack

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Meaning:** References the more-than-$4-billion resolution across authorities; exact totals need source wording.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Separate individual file

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A visibly distinct personal plea folder sits at the other position.

**Meaning:** Preserves the distinction between Binance's plea and Changpeng Zhao's separate Bank Secrecy Act plea.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Vacated executive chair

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** One chair is pushed back from the table.

**Meaning:** Symbolises the leadership resignation associated with the resolution, not an invented departure scene.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Opening-day ledger

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A worn ledger repeats the new-exchange order-book motif from Binance's launch.

**Meaning:** Connects institutional consequences to the earlier growth story without implying inevitability.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [040 — Binance Launches](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/040-binance-launches.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not call the order an endorsement of bitcoin, confuse approval with first trading, or assume every product has identical terms or risks.

# 092 — Dencun

**Date:** 2024-03-13T13:55:35Z  ·  **Era:** Recovery and institutions

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2024-013` → `LORE-CAN-2024-013` / `C2024-013`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

Ethereum's Dencun upgrade activated at epoch 269,568, introducing EIP-4844 blob transactions for cheaper temporary rollup data storage.

**Why it earns a card:** Blob transactions change Ethereum rollup economics.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** Ethereum rollup data gains a new temporary storage lane, reducing the burden of sending that data through the old route. Make the new blobs feel like labelled data bundles with expiry, not permanent archives or cryptocurrency coins.

**Composition:** A transit depot with two data routes; the new temporary lane is spacious and clearly marked.

**Mood:** Practical capacity upgrade; soft cyan inside violet infrastructure.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not say all Ethereum fees vanished, execution moved into blobs, or blob data remains permanently stored by the protocol.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Temporary data crate

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A rounded crate carries an expiry-tag motif.

**Meaning:** References the temporary availability of blob data, distinct from permanent execution history.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Rollup loading dock

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small rollup vehicle uses the new lane while ordinary transactions have another route.

**Meaning:** Connects EIP-4844 to rollup data rather than claiming every transaction uses the same new format.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Reusable shelf space

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** An emptied shelf is ready for a later bundle.

**Meaning:** A metaphor for temporary storage capacity, not a literal deletion schedule.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Governance envelope rack

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** An envelope rack echoes the Arbitrum claim scene near the loading dock.

**Meaning:** Connects an L2 community to the underlying Ethereum upgrade without implying this upgrade belongs only to Arbitrum.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [087 — The Arbitrum Airdrop](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/087-the-arbitrum-airdrop.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

# 093 — The Fourth Halving

**Date:** 2024-04-20  ·  **Era:** Recovery and institutions

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2024-018` → `LORE-CAN-2024-018` / `C2024-018`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

At block 840,000, Bitcoin's programmed block subsidy fell from 6.25 BTC to 3.125 BTC.

**Why it earns a card:** The supply schedule reaches a milestone in the ETF era.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A Bitcoin mining conveyor reaches its fourth scheduled reward reduction while the network keeps working. Emphasise a smaller new subsidy beside the ongoing transaction stream rather than a machine cutting existing holdings in half.

**Composition:** Close view of the reward outlet with a broad working network behind it.

**Mood:** Ritual continuity; amber dawn and disciplined mechanical rhythm.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not halve wallet balances, promise a price rise, or use a US-local date without distinguishing it from the UTC date.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Fourth notch

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A maintenance plate carries four subtle completed notches.

**Meaning:** References the fourth subsidy halving, not four arbitrary calendar years.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Separate fee tray

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small fee tray remains distinct from the newly reduced subsidy outlet.

**Meaning:** Shows that the block subsidy and transaction fees are different components of mining rewards.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Continuous belt

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** The conveyor runs smoothly through the reduction point.

**Meaning:** Represents a scheduled protocol event rather than a market shutdown.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Old half-sized mould

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** An older reward mould from the first-halving brief rests on a shelf.

**Meaning:** Links the 2012 and 2024 reductions while keeping their reward amounts and dates distinct.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [015 — The First Halving](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/015-the-first-halving.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

# 094 — Mt. Gox Repayments

**Date:** 2024-07-05  ·  **Era:** Recovery and institutions

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

## The moment

The Mt. Gox rehabilitation trustee announced that repayments in Bitcoin and Bitcoin Cash had been made to some rehabilitation creditors through designated exchanges.

**Why it earns a card:** A long-running collapse gains a partial resolution a decade later.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** An old creditor opens a repayment notice a decade after an exchange's collapse. A partial queue of envelopes moves through designated exchanges, while many files remain on the table; the story is the beginning of repayments.

**Composition:** A worn original claim folder beneath a fresh notice, with a modest human reaction rather than a victory crowd.

**Mood:** Long-awaited movement with unresolved weight; rain-grey warmed by morning light.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not imply every creditor was fully repaid on 5 July, call these newly mined coins, or merge initial missing amounts with repayment totals.

## Four Easter eggs

### 2. Intermediary routing stamp

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small exchange-routing stamp sits on the envelope.

**Meaning:** Reflects distribution through designated exchanges rather than a universal direct transfer process.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Worn folder corners

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Years of handling show on the original claim folder.

**Meaning:** Makes waiting visible without inventing a specific person's history or recovery amount.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Dried rain receipt

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

## The moment

Bitcoin rose above $100,000 for the first time on 5 December 2024; Reuters reported an intraday high of $103,649.

**Why it earns a card:** Bitcoin crosses the widely recognised $100,000 threshold.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A long-watched bitcoin price display reaches six figures for the first time. A small crowd reacts differently—wonder, disbelief, calculation—while the display remains a historical snapshot rather than a prediction.

**Composition:** Large six-position display reflected in ordinary faces; limit coin spectacle so the number retains meaning.

**Mood:** Astonishment and perspective; warm amber on a dark city evening.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not imply every venue crossed at the same second, use the milestone as today's price, or portray future gains as guaranteed.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. New digit position

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A newly illuminated leading digit contrasts with the older display housing.

**Meaning:** References crossing the $100,000 threshold without claiming a specific exchange tick.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Archived timestamp sleeve

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small date sleeve fixes the display as a historical record.

**Meaning:** Prevents the price from reading as live market information.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Different reactions

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A tiny reflection shows an observer closing a calculator while another simply watches.

**Meaning:** Acknowledges varied personal experiences rather than universal profit.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Four-digit receipt

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A faded four-figure milestone receipt rests beneath the display.

**Meaning:** Links the $1,000 and $100,000 moments without suggesting a guaranteed path between them.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

# 096 — The Presidential Meme Coin

**Date:** 2025-01-17  ·  **Era:** Recovery and institutions

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

## The moment

President-elect Donald Trump promoted the official TRUMP token in a Truth Social post on 17 January 2025, three days before his inauguration. The token was issued on Solana.

**Why it earns a card:** A sitting-president-to-be launches a personal meme token before inauguration.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A president-elect's branded meme token appears on social feeds days before inauguration, merging political attention with speculative token culture. Show an announcement being seen, not a state-backed currency being issued.

**Composition:** A phone announcement in foreground, a distant ceremonial venue kept symbolic and secondary.

**Mood:** Surprise, controversy and spectacle; restrained patriotic colours against market-screen light.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not call it legal tender, a government reserve asset or official national currency; distinguish US-local announcement time from the following UTC date.

# 097 — The Pardon

**Date:** 2025-01-21  ·  **Era:** Recovery and institutions

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-ADD-2025-ROSS` → `LORE-CAN-2025-011` / `ADD-2025-ROSS`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

President Donald Trump granted Ross William Ulbricht a pardon on 21 January 2025. Ulbricht had been serving a life sentence following his 2015 conviction in the Silk Road case.

**Why it earns a card:** The Silk Road founder's pardon closes an important chapter of crypto history.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A signed presidential pardon changes the legal fate of Ross Ulbricht after years in prison. Centre the dated legal document and an opening threshold, avoiding a victory declaration about the underlying conduct.

**Composition:** Document in foreground, a symbolic open doorway beyond; any portrait requires an approved reference.

**Mood:** Relief and contested legacy; quiet daylight after institutional shadow.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not equate a pardon with a judicial finding of innocence, erase the conviction history, or depict a specific release scene without evidence.

**Meaning:** Anchors the act of clemency to the selected 2025 date.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Earlier sentence file

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Meaning:** Preserves the legal history without merging sentencing and pardon dates.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Open threshold shadow

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Light crosses an open doorway but the person remains outside the frame.

**Meaning:** Symbolises changed legal circumstances, not a claim about the actual release location or timing.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Library bookmark returned

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A library-style bookmark echoes the Silk Road shutdown card.

**Meaning:** Connects the arrest-era story to the later pardon while leaving the earlier artwork parked.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

# 098 — The Bybit Heist

**Date:** 2025-02-21  ·  **Era:** Recovery and institutions

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2025-002` → `LORE-CAN-2025-002` / `C2025-002`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

Attackers stole approximately $1.5 billion in virtual assets from Bybit after compromising a signing workflow associated with an external multisignature service.

**Why it earns a card:** A major exchange theft brings signing and custody risk back into focus.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A custody signing desk approves what appears to be a routine transfer while the underlying transaction route has been maliciously altered. The scene centres a compromised signing workflow rather than a crack in Ethereum itself.

**Composition:** Hands at a multisignature console, with the apparent route and concealed route visually distinct but not executable.

**Mood:** Deception and sudden alarm; cold steel with one misleading green approval light.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not provide exploit instructions, attribute blame to an unsupported individual, or claim Ethereum consensus was broken.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Multiple approval seats

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Several distinct approval positions face the same transaction display.

**Meaning:** References the multisignature custody workflow without asserting an unchecked signer threshold.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Mismatched route layers

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A transparent display overlay differs from the route etched beneath it.

**Meaning:** A metaphor for the reported signing-workflow compromise; exact technical mechanism remains a verification item before detailed art.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. False green reflection

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A green confirmation lamp reflects red in a polished surface.

**Meaning:** A visual clue that an apparently routine approval can conceal a different outcome.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Independent screen reminder

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A small device-shaped sketch recalls Keys in Your Pocket.

**Meaning:** A thematic link to transaction verification and key security, not a claim a particular consumer device would certainly have prevented the incident.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [029 — Keys in Your Pocket](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/029-keys-in-your-pocket.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

# 099 — The US Bitcoin Reserve

**Date:** 2025-03-06  ·  **Era:** Recovery and institutions

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2025-004` → `LORE-CAN-2025-004` / `C2025-004`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

A US executive order established a Strategic Bitcoin Reserve capitalized initially with finally forfeited government BTC and directed that Bitcoin deposited into the reserve not be sold.

**Why it earns a card:** An executive order establishes a federal strategic Bitcoin reserve.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** An executive order establishes a US strategic bitcoin reserve using eligible forfeited holdings as its initial basis. Show an inventory being redesignated, with other digital assets kept in a separate stockpile file.

**Composition:** Government records room with a newly labelled bitcoin ledger and a distinct secondary asset cabinet.

**Mood:** Institutional consequence; sober navy and restrained amber.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not depict an automatic market-buying spree, a new national digital currency, or all seized assets as already finally forfeited and freely usable.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

### 1. Forfeiture status stamp

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** An inventory line is marked finally forfeited rather than merely seized.

**Meaning:** References the legal status of holdings specified for the initial reserve.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 2. Separate stockpile cabinet

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** Other digital-asset folders sit in a visibly separate cabinet.

**Meaning:** Distinguishes the bitcoin reserve from the order's separate digital-asset stockpile.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

### 3. Relabelled shelf

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A new reserve label is fitted to an existing inventory shelf.

**Meaning:** A metaphor for an institutional holding-policy change, not proof of a fresh purchase.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Different tender notice

**Type:** Collection crossover. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A folded legal-tender notice from the El Salvador scene sits in a comparison folder.

**Meaning:** Connects state adoption stories while explicitly distinguishing a reserve from a legal-tender regime.

**Evidence:** Intentional collection reference, not an object documented at the event.

**Collection link:** [071 — Bitcoin Becomes Legal Tender](https://github.com/d4np4yn3-netizen/Lore/blob/main/cards/crypto/season-01/briefs/v1/071-bitcoin-becomes-legal-tender.md). Intentional crossover; a forward reference is anachronistic.

# 100 — The GENIUS Act

**Date:** 2025-07-18  ·  **Era:** Recovery and institutions

**Brief:** Draft for Dan’s review. New moment/scene proposal; no artwork approval.

**Production hold:** No hold recorded in selection v1; artwork still requires its normal review.

**Record:** `BRIEF-C2025-008` → `LORE-CAN-2025-008` / `C2025-008`. Review order is not collector numbering.

## The moment

The US President signed S. 1582, the Guiding and Establishing National Innovation for U.S. Stablecoins Act (GENIUS Act), into law.

**Why it earns a card:** US stablecoin legislation becomes a federal milestone.

## Illustration brief

**Scene:** A federal payment-stablecoin framework is signed into law, creating a rulebook around a specific class of digital money. Keep the book's scope visibly narrower than the entire crypto market and its rollout unfinished.

**Composition:** Signed law in foreground, a stablecoin payment desk beyond, with a partly assembled implementation calendar.

**Mood:** A new regulatory chapter; cream, navy and measured green.

**Factual boundaries:** Do not say all crypto was approved, all stablecoins instantly complied, or every provision became operational on the signing day.

## Four Easter eggs

Locations are illustration concepts unless explicitly marked as retained. Meaning and historical status must remain clear in the artist handoff.

**Meaning:** Keeps the statutory scope distinct from all tokens and blockchain applications.

**Evidence:** Inherited event-research lead. Check the specific claim against the listed sources before final text/art; placement is illustrative.

**Type:** Historical reference. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

### 3. Narrow bookend

**Type:** Symbolic detail. **Status:** CONCEPT_FOR_REVIEW.

**Where:** A bookend encloses only the stablecoin section of a much larger financial shelf.

**Meaning:** A visual reminder that one law does not settle every crypto legal question.

**Evidence:** Creative interpretation; no claim that this prop historically existed.

### 4. Old token claim stub
